# Seed 64 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - the JIT back, the small end the best yet, I+ and the loop compiler everywhere (Iteration 443)

`seed 64 10h widths noevolved then experiment footprint.py --fastest --sample 16 --sizes 100,10000 --rounds 3` at
c094ab788 (Iteration 442): free, the after-seed-63 batch and the JIT fix applied. The remote checks started 03:16:06,
the evolution 03:21:51; it evolved 9 h 25 min. 37,499 designs, generations 0-146: 28,227 alive, 9,272 screened (25.5%),
**not one died** - no compile failure, no reach-limit refusal, no 32-bit failure.

**No verdict** - no comparison step. **By its own measures**: fastest 0.125 (seed 62: 0.116, seed 63: 0.263), smallest
**14,047 bytes - the smallest of any run** (seed 63: 14,348, seed 58: 14,146); at 32 bits its fastest 23a0fa97de 0.333.
**The JIT is back**: on 81 of 167 front designs, jitx and jitloop on all 81, jitinl on 76 (seed 63: 10, the fix's
point). **The rtloopall repair worked**: rtloopall expressed in 24,364 of the 31,395 genomes that set it (78%; seed 62:
1.6%), on 155 of the front; rtiplus, behind it, on 55 (33%; seed 63: 0).

**Facts.** Its front: 167 designs, 81 tag-2; tos on 98. heldout (sieve): Spearman 0.978 - the fastest tenth 0.888, the
pick's regret +0.0%, the best yet. Fast-end optimism 1.085 (the fast gate's rule: revisit). The best speed's last 2% gain
at generation 36; the smallest still gaining at 143 of 146. **The footprint check** (its 16 fastest, 100 and 10,000
words): the selection's order holds at 10,000 words - Spearman 0.888 (0.797 at 100) - their lead over s6 halving, from
3-5x to about 2x. The rules (review-checks.py rules): the freeze, 32-bit, the small end and I+ PASS; the fast gate
ATTENTION; rtimm/rtfuse - none relies on the overlap: rtfuse to govern, proposed. Its chain's remote checks, of seed 63's
front: results/xcheck-seed64.md.

---
# Evolved VM designs

28227 designs evaluated, 28227 alive, 9272 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): 9c51381240, speed 0.130, binary + image 29,830, memory 180 KB - Q 0.212 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.

## The footprint check (the chain's experiment step)

pinned to cpu 4 (it and its sibling 11% busy; BENCH_CPU=N to choose)
# Footprint: does the front's speed hold as the code grows? (lab/evolve/footprint.py)

Front: build/evolve/db.jsonl, re-measured front (167 designs); sampled 16 by selection speed - the fastest, fastest to slowest. Sizes 100, 10000; calls 40 a word, at least 200,000; 3 rounds, medians.

| 100 | 25450 | 114.1 |
| 10,000 | 337946 | 205.5 |
